It has really strained our relationship, but I haven't taken any extreme steps like hiring an investigator or filing lawsuits.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any hallucinations, such as hearing voices that others don't hear, in addition to your delusional beliefs about your family's plot?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there, and my senses are completely normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any specific behaviors or actions your family members have exhibited that you believe are evidence of their plot against you, and how you interpret these actions?\n\nPatient: Whenever there are minor disagreements or delays in signing the legal paperwork for my parents' house in Mexico, I know it is a deliberate stall tactic. Even the emails and documents they show me are just a front; my ex-husband, the kids, and their lawyer are only pretending to include me while secretly plotting to cut me out of all family decisions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility, even for a moment, that your family might not be plotting against you, or is this belief something you're absolutely certain about all the time?"

My concerns are strictly about my ex-husband, the kids, and their lawyer trying to cut me out of my parents' house. Outside of this family mess, I manage my office team and volunteer shifts completely normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on your belief that your family is plotting against you, such as hiring a private investigator, confronting them directly, or taking legal action?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have confronted them directly about it, which always leads to tense arguments where I try to get them to just admit what they're doing. It has really strained our relationship, but I haven't taken any extreme steps like hiring an investigator or filing lawsuits.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any hallucinations, such as hearing voices that others don't hear, in addition to your delusional beliefs about your family's plot?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there, and my senses are completely normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any specific behaviors or actions your family members have exhibited that you believe are evidence of their plot against you, and how you interpret these actions?\n\nPatient: Whenever there are minor disagreements or delays in signing the legal paperwork for my parents' house in Mexico, I know it is a deliberate stall tactic. Even the emails and documents they show me are just a front; my ex-husband, the kids, and their lawyer are only pretending to include me while secretly plotting to cut me out of all family decisions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility, even for a moment, that your family might not be plotting against you, or is this belief something you're absolutely certain about all the time?\n\nPatient: No, I am absolutely certain all the time and have never doubted it for a single second. All of their explanations and the documents they show me are clearly just a cover-up to hide what they are actually planning. I know my family, and it is completely obvious to me that they are working behind my back.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your daily life or routines that are specifically influenced by your belief about your family's plot, aside from avoiding family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Outside of family matters, my daily routines are completely unaffected; I still manage my office team and do my volunteer shifts at the senior center without any issues. However, whenever a routine task or conversation involves my ex-husband or my children, I get so focused on their plotting that the interaction becomes incredibly tense, argumentative, and exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you think is the ultimate goal of your family's supposed plot against you, and what do you believe they hope to achieve by cutting you out?\n\nPatient: I am convinced their ultimate goal is to completely undermine my influence and push me out of any decisions regarding my parents' house and family belongings. They want to control the estate and hide their real legal plans from me, using those papers and emails just to keep me quiet. It is incredibly exhausting to know they are doing this just to take away my say in my own family's legacy."
  }

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: My family keeps telling me everything is normal, but I’m absolutely convinced they’re secretly working together behind my back. I just can’t shake this belief, no matter what kind of proof or papers they try to show me to convince me otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your behavior or emotional state, such as becoming easily irritated, anxious, or detached from reality, in addition to these persistent thoughts about your family?\n\nPatient: I do get very tense and argumentative whenever I have to interact with my family, and I've started avoiding family gatherings altogether because of it. However, outside of my family, I am functioning completely normally—I still run my office team successfully and do my volunteer shifts without any issues at all. I don't feel detached from reality, just deeply concerned about what they are planning behind my back.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how long you've held this belief about your family, and has it evolved over time into a more complex or detailed theory, or has it remained relatively consistent?\n\nPatient: This has been going on for about eleven months now, starting right when we began having issues with selling my parents' house and sorting out their papers. The core belief that my ex-husband and kids are working to cut me out has stayed the same, but it has definitely hardened over time. Now, even minor delays in signing forms or small disagreements just feel like more proof of their plot.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes where your belief about your family's plot seemed to momentarily fade or weaken, only to return later, or has it been consistently present and unshakeable since it started?\n\nPatient: No, it has never faded or weakened; it has been completely constant and unshakeable for the entire eleven months. Even when they show me emails or legal documents to prove they aren't hiding anything, I just see those papers as part of their cover-up. I have never doubted for a second that they are working against me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other delusional beliefs or ideas outside of your family, such as concerns about being followed, poisoned, or having special powers?\n\nPatient: No, absolutely not, my mind is completely clear and I don't experience any strange things like being followed or poisoned. My concerns are strictly about my ex-husband, the kids, and their lawyer trying to cut me out of my parents' house. Outside of this family mess, I manage my office team and volunteer shifts completely normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any actions based on your belief that your family is plotting against you, such as hiring a private investigator, confronting them directly, or taking legal action?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have confronted them directly about it, which always leads to tense arguments where I try to get them to just admit what they're doing. It has really strained our relationship, but I haven't taken any extreme steps like hiring an investigator or filing lawsuits.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any hallucinations, such as hearing voices that others don't hear, in addition to your delusional beliefs about your family's plot?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there, and my senses are completely normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any specific behaviors or actions your family members have exhibited that you believe are evidence of their plot against you, and how you interpret these actions?\n\nPatient: Whenever there are minor disagreements or delays in signing the legal paperwork for my parents' house in Mexico, I know it is a deliberate stall tactic. Even the emails and documents they show me are just a front; my ex-husband, the kids, and their lawyer are only pretending to include me while secretly plotting to cut me out of all family decisions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility, even for a moment, that your family might not be plotting against you, or is this belief something you're absolutely certain about all the time?\n\nPatient: No, I am absolutely certain all the time and have never doubted it for a single second. All of their explanations and the documents they show me are clearly just a cover-up to hide what they are actually planning. I know my family, and it is completely obvious to me that they are working behind my back.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your daily life or routines that are specifically influenced by your belief about your family's plot, aside from avoiding family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Outside of family matters, my daily routines are completely unaffected; I still manage my office team and do my volunteer shifts at the senior center without any issues. However, whenever a routine task or conversation involves my ex-husband or my children, I get so focused on their plotting that the interaction becomes incredibly tense, argumentative, and exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you think is the ultimate goal of your family's supposed plot against you, and what do you believe they hope to achieve by cutting you out?\n\nPatient: I am convinced their ultimate goal is to completely undermine my influence and push me out of any decisions regarding my parents' house and family belongings. They want to control the estate and hide their real legal plans from me, using those papers and emails just to keep me quiet. It is incredibly exhausting to know they are doing this just to take away my say in my own family's legacy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any periods where you felt your belief about your family's plot was less intense or you were less preoccupied with it, even if it didn't completely go away?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any periods where it felt less intense or went away. Ever since this started eleven months ago, it has been a constant, unshakeable conviction that they are plotting against me. I am always on edge and preoccupied whenever any family matters come up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a second opinion or an independent assessment of the situation with your family's estate and legal matters to help clarify any potential misunderstandings or miscommunications?"
  }

I haven't really thought about getting an independent legal assessment or a second opinion. I feel so completely sure about what they are doing that I just didn't think it would make any difference anyway.

However, the patient's otherwise normal functioning outside of family matters and lack of other delusional beliefs or hallucinations also raises the possibility of a brief psychotic disorder, although the duration of eleven months makes this less likely. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nMy family keeps telling me everything is normal, but I’m absolutely convinced they’re secretly working together behind my back. I just can’t shake this belief, no matter what kind of proof or papers they try to show me to convince me otherwise.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate.
